* HIERREP chain, one mechanical stop and its repair (2026-11-13): the chain stopped at 15:58:02Z at the gate of DAG_s0 (selfcheck refused: the trainer's eval-disjointness check K-B3 needs the eval grid at its repository path inside the tree, and the pod tree export held only stack/ and taniteval/). GPU idle for about 5 minutes. The stop record is kept on the pod as hierrep/STAGEB_STOP_1_GATE-DAG_s0.txt. HIER_s0 and FLAT_s0 were finished before the stop and are untouched.
* PI decisions (2026-11-13, in chat, verbatim: 'three at once, lean 4: run on your self+'): (1) three training arms at once on the pod are allowed (this lifts, for this fork's pod queue, the standing rule against adding load to a pod that is training and against evaluating on it); (2) the lead installs Lean 4 on the pod itself. Realised as: the HIERREP chain stays as it is, and the SPATIAL and DATAEFF chains are released to run beside it (one arm each, so three arms at once); fall-back rule fixed before the release: if any arm runs under 3.5 steps/s after 5 minutes (one arm alone: 4.7 to 4.8), the DATAEFF chain is stopped first. No outcome rule of any card changes; seeds, gates, pins and arms files are unchanged.
* Release of the SPATIAL and DATAEFF queues beside HIERREP (2026-11-13), two mechanical stops and their repairs. First release 18:20 to 18:21Z: both queues stopped at their first real-data gate before any training step. (a) SPATIAL, gate G_s0: RuntimeError in WMAux.probe_state of the patched model file (the probe input was built on the CPU while the model was on the GPU; the dev-box checks of the patch ran with the GPU hidden, so they could not see it). Repair: one line, the probe input is moved to the device of the projection (spatial/stage1/SPATIAL_fix1.patch; patched v8f_mini.py sha256 687a41030e8dc193, before bbc627a0845086a3; the pin in pod/chain2/arms_spatial.txt follows, arms file sha256 5ba81ef95e847d1f). The probe is a gate check; no training code path changes. (b) DATAEFF, gate R1_4_s0: the token tool of the HIERREP chain refused the arm because it holds the HIERREP counts (4,512 clips, 60 excluded) as constants. Repair: pod/chain2/gate_token2.py (sha256 e1f6b8287674a0b6) = the same tool with the two counts as arguments (defaults 4,512 and 60), and chain_arms.sh (sha256 380563682b285a81) passes each arm's declared counts; regression check before the upload: defaults accept the HIER_s0 selfcheck, the counts 1,128 and 3,444 on the same selfcheck are refused. The HIERREP chain and its copy of the tool are not touched. Hashes equal on the dev box and the pod; both arms files pass the dry run. Second release 18:27:13Z (SPATIAL) and 18:27:15Z (DATAEFF). No outcome rule, seed, pin of data or arm definition changes.
* Lean 4 on the pod (PI decision above): install started by the lead 18:27:37Z (lean/install_lean.sh sha256 8a283377aac14b61, equal on both sides; elan 4.2.4, toolchain of Mathlib: Lean v4.36.0-rc2; CPU and disk only, nice 19). While the Mathlib cache was unpacked the running arm slowed (FLAT_s1: 50 steps in about 35 s near 18:36Z); a one-time cost of the install.
* HIER seed 1 read automatically (2026-11-13, eval bank 18:53 local, train probe set 19:03 local; controls first, all pass in both): spatial/s1/VERDICT_S1EVAL_HIER_s1.md, VERDICT_S1_HIER_s1.md; two-seed spread spatial/s1/SPREAD_S1_HIER_2seeds.md.
* Three arms at once, start and first speed window (2026-11-13). The second release (18:27Z) made no progress for 11 minutes: the two gates ran at the same moment, each with about 115 threads on a pod limited to 7.65 cores, and starved each other and the running arm (a gate alone takes 12 to 13 s; FLAT_s1 fell under 1 step/s). At 18:38:35Z the lead stopped the DATAEFF queue (as the fall-back rule orders it: DATAEFF first); the SPATIAL gate then passed in 17 s. Repair: pod/chain2/chain_arms.sh, third version (sha256 1c57dae4f8159622): one CPU-heavy phase (gate, T1-kin dump) at a time across the queues that use this script (a shared lock), the gate's selfcheck with 4 threads, and a time limit with two retries for a phase with no result (gate 900 s, dump 2,700 s). Training calls and the environment of the dump are not changed. Both queues restarted on this version (SPATIAL lost 350 steps of G_s0; its partial run folder, 4 small files, was removed on the pod): gate G_s0 18:41:32Z to 18:41:45Z, training since 18:41:45Z; gate R1_4_s0 passed 18:42:44Z (with the declared counts 1,128 clips and 3,444 excluded), training since 18:42:44Z. Three arms at once since 18:42:44Z. First speed window, 18:47:54Z to 18:52:54Z (steps from train_log.jsonl): FLAT_s1 950 steps (3.17 steps/s), G_s0 900 steps (3.02), R1_4_s0 950 steps (3.17); together 9.3 steps/s against 4.7 to 4.8 for one arm alone. All three are under the 3.5 of the fall-back rule. The window held a fourth load started by the lead (the Lean project build, 18:47:36Z to 18:48:26Z, idle priority). Written at 18:54Z before any further reading: ONE confirmation window, 18:55:00Z to 19:00:00Z, with no other job of this fork on the pod, decides; the rule is applied to it as written (any arm under 3.5 steps/s: the DATAEFF queue is stopped and re-armed to start when the SPATIAL queue is done); no third window.
* Confirmation window and the rule, applied as written (2026-11-13): 18:55:01Z to 19:00:01Z, no other job of this fork on the pod: FLAT_s1 950 steps (3.2 steps/s), G_s0 950 (3.2), R1_4_s0 950 (3.2); together 9.5 steps/s. Every arm is under 3.5, so the fall-back rule applies: the DATAEFF queue was stopped at 19:01:13Z. The same readings showed the cause, a defect of the queue script of this fork: chain_hierrep.sh runs every arm with OMP, MKL and OPENBLAS threads 6 (its lines 98 to 100); chain_arms.sh set none of them, so G_s0 and R1_4_s0 ran with 163 threads each (FLAT_s1: 21) at 2.5 cores each (FLAT_s1: 1.3), the 7.65-core limit was the brake, and the two arms were not in the process environment of the HIERREP arms they are read against. For that second reason the SPATIAL queue was stopped at the same time (G_s0 at step 3,350 and R1_4_s0 at step 3,300 are discarded; no result of either was read). Repair: chain_arms.sh, fourth version (sha256 fba26899abaac579): the same three thread settings, PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE, PYTHONUNBUFFERED and the PYTHONPATH of the arm's own tree, as chain_hierrep.sh sets them; the gate's own 4-thread setting of the third version is dropped; the shared lock and the time limits stay. No argv, seed, pin of data or outcome rule changes. Written at 19:02Z, before the restart and before any speed reading of the repaired queues: both queues restart on the fourth version, three arms at once again (the PI's decision; the miss above was measured on a defective configuration). The same fall-back rule holds for ONE window of 5 minutes that starts 5 minutes after the third arm trains, with no other job of this fork on the pod and with no confirmation window: any arm under 3.5 steps/s and the DATAEFF queue is stopped and re-armed to start when the SPATIAL queue is done.
* The deciding window on the repaired queues (2026-11-13; both queues restarted on the fourth version: gate G_s0 19:02:03Z to 19:02:15Z, gate R1_4_s0 passed 19:02:29Z; 21 threads per trainer, as FLAT_s1): 19:07:30Z to 19:12:31Z, no other job of this fork on the pod (the puller of this fork was stopped at 19:03Z for the window and restarted after it). G_s0 1,450 steps (4.9 steps/s), R1_4_s0 1,350 steps (4.6 steps/s): no arm under 3.5, so by the rule as written the DATAEFF queue keeps running. Before the repair the same two arms ran at 3.2. What the window did NOT hold: a third TRAINING arm. FLAT_s1 had finished its 19,164 steps at 19:05Z; in the window the HIERREP chain ran its T1-kin dump and then the bank build of HIER_s1 (four processes, about 3.8 cores, and the GPU). So the state 'three training arms' is not yet measured on the repaired queues. Written at 19:13Z before any such reading: the same rule also holds, as written, for the first 5-minute window that starts 5 minutes after the next HIERREP training arm (DAG_s1) begins; that window is read by a script on the pod (pod/chain2/speed3.sh) and a miss stops the DATAEFF queue. This adds a condition under which the queue is stopped; it removes none.

2026-11-20T10:18:09Z | FAULT OF THE READ TOOL FOUND AFTER THE READ OF CARD ADD70 (row 128 landed in hold 92, 99313c08): the family reader and the headway reader take the speed, heading, yaw rate and time gap of step 1 from the displacement between the frame origin (0,0) and the first track point (HEADWAY_DEFS: p_0 = (0,0)); the wrapper of card ADD70 writes the track of a pre-rolled window in the LOGGED frame at t0, so that track starts at the carried offset and not at the origin. Probe on the pod (podread/probe/probe_p0.py, read-only, seed 0): first point from the origin, median 2.10 m in the fresh run RLcKF5Hb20 and 1.99 m in the state-carry control, against 6.02 m in RLcKF5P30Hb20 and 6.19 m in R0KNlP30Hb20 with a second step of 2.0 m; the first step exceeds twice the second plus 0.5 m in 0.595 and 0.585 of the aged windows and in 0.000 of the fresh and the carry windows. Affected in every pair with an aged run of row 128: the unsafe rate U (its time gap divides by the step speed), speed error, heading error, yaw-rate error, curvature and the manoeuvre classes. Not affected: along-track and cross-track error, tactical track error, contact, falling behind, the route goal of the strategic instrument (positions only). Control l3 could not see it (a pre-roll on the logged track has offset 0). Root-cause class: an instrument's implicit frame assumption (the window starts at the origin of the frame it is scored in) broken by a change of the frame the wrapper writes in; the wrapper's frame mapping was checked, the reader's use of it was not. Until the re-read the affected readings of row 128 are not quoted; the other session is told now; the read of card ADD71 waits for the repaired reader (no value of card ADD71 opened).
